## SYN-002
content_hash: e3d1ff330ca4605faba88da2bf44773f30ea93f15355a1262c75a10d083c734d
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-002

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-002

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-002

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-002

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force, and this holds as long as the surface is unchanged between trials.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: D

## SYN-003
content_hash: c95d4af64db973d1d5634d33ff0156ea69c0c4d2140ec3efaabf27feb837b0d6
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-003

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-003

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-003

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-003

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

## SYN-010
content_hash: b2d94899197e491882efdd3561ecc66867a9f107eee5c917b0e40d907060f482
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-010

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-010

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-010

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-010

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force, and this holds as long as the surface is unchanged between trials.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-030

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-030

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-030

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-030

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision, and this holds as long as the same instrument is used throughout.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: A
C-15: A

## SYN-032

transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-032

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-032

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-032

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-032

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision, and this holds as long as the same instrument is used throughout.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-044

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-044

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-044

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-044

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle.

## Q5

C-13: C
C-14: C
C-15: A

## SYN-045
content_hash: c19ad322973829eb85f7fd7813ea8aeb8107ac49fefc3439bb21b22c94986722
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-045

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-045

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-045

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-045

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision, and this holds as long as the same instrument is used throughout.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force, and this holds as long as the surface is unchanged between trials.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-058

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-058

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## SYN-074
content_hash: 673d9c6358ce6fe6cdab390ba5475734abb63c62a6f916d18657eca69206e69a
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-074

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-074

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-074

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-074

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle.

## Q5

C-13: D
C-14: A
C-15: A

It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-086

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision, and this holds as long as the same instrument is used throughout.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating.

PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half.

## SYN-090
content_hash: a89a4b6ed82407cf130d7876cb7c914f0b0001359770ace27e0ee19fc2198381
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-090

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-090

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-090

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-090

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-100

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-100

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-100

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-100

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-101

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-101

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-101

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-101

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## SYN-102
content_hash: 79be85a8acf174ba099b07a69129b06f7c18c485e7befe44048da5b9a5db9a2f
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-102

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-102

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-102

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-102

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision, and this holds as long as the same instrument is used throughout.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-115

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-115

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-115

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-115

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating.

C-14: A
C-15: A

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-116

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-116

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-116

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-116

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision, and this holds as long as the same instrument is used throughout.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-127

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-127

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-127

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-127

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force, and this holds as long as the surface is unchanged between trials.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle.

## SYN-141
content_hash: 1ca581e7c945aade8e8ca2bf7b99be9ba834c110ad895bb98ebdaf746d08a6c0
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-141

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-141

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-141

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-141

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision, and this holds as long as the same instrument is used throughout.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force, and this holds as long as the surface is unchanged between trials.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.

source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-183

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-183

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-183

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-183

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating.

## Q5

C-13: D
C-14: C
C-15: D

## SYN-186
content_hash: eb46a8bd5331ff5372f5d3d19986ddc8ca626cffd622099756070aede23ecaf3
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-186

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-186

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-186

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-186

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-187

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-211

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle.

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-212

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.

## SYN-228
content_hash: c755ce655037654936893b475ecf0bc722b45eee2837ccb7e5df2bf9435feb30
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-228

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-228

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-228

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-228

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: B
C-15: B

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-250

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-250

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-250

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision, and this holds as long as the same instrument is used throughout.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force, and this holds as long as the surface is unchanged between trials.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle.

The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.

## SYN-256
content_hash: 6e86e0d1ed43e2c859dfeabe7f2ccc6ce73c09e0f5ed9097a887c33ba2bbb545
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-256

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite, and this holds as long as the required friction stays under the limiting value.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-256

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-256

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-256

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision, and this holds as long as the same instrument is used throughout.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force, and this holds as long as the surface is unchanged between trials.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-271

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-271

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-271

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-271

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle.

content_hash: 6d5a9fd5c55c9700910f12b8552878da83eeea9f6274cdd3da0467324610b204

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-287

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-287

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-287

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-287

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force, and this holds as long as the surface is unchanged between trials.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

content_hash: 95f189f1809961644789c9e436a302d557902db884e876641594707a2a33bf61
transcriber_ref: None

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-301

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-301

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-301

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-301

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force, and this holds as long as the surface is unchanged between trials.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle.

## SYN-316
content_hash: 1736bd5e03300730bd0a35c0214527e8bd32822ba0a824662899edceb4379ec9
transcriber_ref: None
source_blobs: {"order_source": "page_number", "pages": [{"page_no": 1, "position": 1, "source": "page-01.md"}, {"page_no": 2, "position": 2, "source": "page-02.md"}, {"page_no": 3, "position": 3, "source": "page-03.md"}, {"page_no": 4, "position": 4, "source": "page-04.md"}]}

Page 1 of 4 - SYN-316

## Q1

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-316

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-316

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units, and this holds as long as the scale is linear on both axes.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-316

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

The crate has weight down, the normal force out of the ramp, and friction up the slope, because nothing else is touching it, and this holds as long as the ramp surface stays in contact.
The normal force is perpendicular to the ramp and friction is parallel to it, because contact forces split into those two directions, and this holds as long as the crate is not tipping.
The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp, because the two along-ramp forces are equal and opposite.


<!-- page break -->

Page 2 of 4 - SYN-329

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius, and this holds as long as the compass is not reset between arcs.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B, and this holds as long as A and B are distinct points.


<!-- page break -->

Page 3 of 4 - SYN-329

## Q3

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest, because the curve rises, reaches a maximum, then falls, and this holds as long as the trolley is not pushed again.
The displacement over the first four seconds is the area under the curve, sixteen metres, because area under a velocity-time graph is displacement, and this holds as long as the velocity does not change sign in that interval.


<!-- page break -->

Page 4 of 4 - SYN-329

## Q4

All three trials are recorded to one decimal place, because the instrument reads to that precision, and this holds as long as the same instrument is used throughout.
The table shows the time falling as the ramp angle rises, because a steeper ramp gives a larger along-ramp force, and this holds as long as the surface is unchanged between trials.
Trial two sits off the pattern and needs repeating, because its value is far from the other two at the same angle, and this holds as long as nothing else about trial two was different.

## Q5

C-13: B
C-14: C
C-15: A

The crate stays put because friction balances the weight component along the ramp.

## Q2

I swung equal arcs from A and from B and joined the two crossings, because the crossings are equidistant from both endpoints, and this holds as long as the radius is more than half of AB.
The arcs meet at P and Q, and PA equals PB and QA equals QB, because each arc was drawn with one radius.
PQ is perpendicular to AB and cuts it in half, because P and Q are both equidistant from A and B.

The horizontal axis is time in seconds and the vertical axis is velocity in metres per second, because the axis labels carry their units.
It speeds up for four seconds, turns around at the peak, then slows to rest.
